  - The level steps: the first comes at the same total EXP it did before the 2026-10-19 curve, the second at the second armour set's level (`Config/Armor`).
  - Each giver has its own `IdleStyle` in `NpcIdleController` (Farmer, Ranger, Explorer) and a portrait in `npc.portrait` (Pip `rbxassetid://111659781861627`).
- **Questline "Trouble on Dale's Farm" (2026-10-08, user's design; saved since 2026-10-09):**
  - Content is all in `Shared.Config.Quests`: 10 steps (kill 5 chickens, **chop 5 Oak Logs**, kill 10 chickens, 3 bulls, level 9, **bring 8 Oak Logs + 8 Stone**, 5 bulls, the boss, level 14, the boss 5 times (the levels were 5 and 8 before the 2026-10-19 curve); steps 2 and 6 were "earn 75 coins" / "give 200 coins" until 2026-10-19, when the user tied gathering in), with rewards, Dale's lines (`offer` / `reminder` / `complete`), the Objective text and the tracker line. In lines `[...]` is the bold red objective; in `track` `{...}` is the red enemy or thing name (user: only the name red, the rest white).
  - The final reward's `weapon` (the Weathervane Spear) is granted at hand-in through `MeleeService.grant`.
  - `QuestService` (server) publishes `QuestStep`, `QuestState` (Offer / Active / Ready / Done) and `QuestProgress`, and takes `Remotes.QuestAction` ("accept" / "turnIn") only within 16 studs of the giver. Kills come from `onDefeated`; "earn" counts rises of `Coins` after accepting; "level" / "give" re-read `Level` / `Coins` (a give step drops back to Active if coins are spent). Hand-in takes the coins for "give", moves on, then pays the reward. **Gathering kinds (2026-10-19):** "gather" (`target` material, `count`) counts rises of that material in the `Materials` attribute after accepting; "bring" (`items` { { id, count } }) re-reads the materials held, each item capped at what it needs (spare logs never stand in for stone), drops back to Active if they're spent, and takes them at hand-in. The tracker and the dialogue's Objective show the material's icon (`Quests.material`); the guide arrow points at the nearest node standing for this player that drops the material still needed (`GatherController.nearest`). **Fish never go in quests** (user: fishing is only for potions).
  - The giver is a model tagged **`QuestGiver`** with `QuestLine = "DaleFarm"` (`Npcs/Farmer`); Rowan (`RangerRedwoods`) and Pip (`ExplorerCaverns`) work the same way. `Client.Controllers.QuestController` gives it a **Talk prompt on E** (it was briefly on F while the enemies' Challenge prompts competed for E). The prompt is drawn by us, not Roblox (Talk prompt **B "speech bubble"**, user's pick 2026-10-10): QuestController sets the prompt's `Style = Custom` and a `ShortName` attribute (Dale), and `Client.Controllers.PromptController` clones `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/UI/TalkPrompt` (or the template named by the prompt's `Template` attribute) on `PromptShown`. Names: `Content` (`PopScale`), `Shadow`, `Tail`, `Bubble/Label` (the gui's `TextFormat` "Talk to %s" with `ShortName` or `ObjectText`), `Key/Label` (keyboard key, gamepad button, or "Tap"), and `Hit` (a click/tap holds the prompt). `PopSeconds` sets the pop in/out. Both labels' boxes are centred (the bubble's in the body, clear of the E badge on the corner: x 0.15–0.85, y 0.17–0.83) with an empty `UIPadding`, and PromptController runs `Util.FontDrop.watch` on each clone (measured centred 2026-10-10). Any future custom prompt can reuse it. Talking hides `WeaponHud` and `QuestTracker`, freezes the player and hides their body, and eases the camera to Dale's face (a Scheduler Camera system, priority 40, re-asserting Scriptable), framed right of the card and swung 18° to his left so the pitchfork clears his face. Hand-in carries straight on into the next offer.
  - `Client.UI.DialogueUI` plays `StarterGui/Dialogue` (Dialogue B "Side card"): types each line at the gui's `CharsPerSecond` (60) with `MaxVisibleGraphemes`; click / E / Space / Enter finishes, then advances. Names: `Window` (`PopScale`, `Skin`, `Portrait/Photo`, `NpcName`, `QuestTag` ("Quest 1 of 10", plain outlined text like the name; no bubble, user's request), `Text`, `Tap`, `NextHint`, `Objective` (`Icon`, `Caption`, `Goal`; attributes `CoinIcon`, `LevelIcon`), `Reward` (`Caption`, and `Chips`: a wrapping UIListLayout row holding `Coins` "25 Coins", `Exp` "20 EXP", `Weapon` chips with `Amount` and `Icon`; a chip that doesn't fit flows onto a second line (user, 2026-10-11, for quests with more rewards); the Weapon chip shows the weapon's drawn icon), `Choices` (`Accept` "Accept Quest" / "Claim Reward" = the Inventory's green button, `Later`, each with `Label`)).
  - `Client.UI.QuestTrackerUI` fills `StarterGui/QuestTracker/Tracker` (**Tracker C "Target badge"**, user's switch 2026-10-08; right side, shown only while Active / Ready): `Tab/Label` "Dale's Farm · 1/10" / "Complete! · 1/10", `Badge` with `Portrait` (enemy portrait, coin, level icon, or Dale once done) and `Ring` (`Track`, plus `Right` / `Left` halves whose hard-edged `UIGradient`s are rotated: right 0–50%, left 50–100%), `Verb` (words before `{}` in `track`, upper-cased), `Target` (the red name), `Count` ("3 / 5", or `returnNote`), and `Chips` for a "bring" step with several materials (option A on https://claude.ai/artifact/D84qqeMAjBMNG7uAoCjxMk, 2026-10-19): one clone of `QuestTracker/Templates/MaterialChip` (`Icon`, `Count` in its `Format`; `ShortFill` / `ShortText` / `DoneFill` / `DoneText` attributes) per item in place of `Count`, and the badge shows the first item still short. State colours are `Tab*` / `Rim*` / `Lip*` / `Fill*` / `Track*` attributes on `Tracker`.
  - **Attribute order matters (bug fixed 2026-10-08):** quests 3, 4, 7, 8 and 10 showed as done right after accepting. The client read the new `QuestStep` paired with the old step's `QuestProgress` / `Ready`, and the tracker's "caught up" latch locked it. Now `QuestService.publish` sends Progress, then State, then Step; `QuestTrackerUI` defers its render to read all three together; and a latch needs server Ready and clears on any step change or non-Active/Ready state. Test each step with the Studio-only `DevQuestStep_<lineId>` player attribute (a bare `DevQuestStep` means Dale's line; set it from the Server datamodel; set it to nil first if it already holds that number).
  - **Shown state:** the tracker publishes `QuestShownState` on the local player (client-only: "Active" until the counters catch up, then "Ready"). `GuideController` follows that, so the arrow no longer turns to Dale before the HUD shows the level or coins.
  - **Talking locks input:** `QuestController` sets the local `InputLocked` attribute and anchors the HumanoidRootPart as well as disabling controls. `WeaponController.swing`, `SprintController` and HotbarUI's key 1 check `InputLocked`. `Dialogue/Catcher` is a full-screen transparent button behind the card: a click anywhere advances, and it never reaches the world.
  - **Progress follows the HUD, not the server (user's report):** the server adds coins / EXP the moment an enemy dies, but the counters wait for the coins and stars to fly in. So "earn" / "give" steps show `CoinsUI.shown()` and "level" steps `PlayerCardUI.Exp.shownLevel()` (both have `onShown` signals), and a step only turns green once the counter has caught up. Kill steps update immediately.
  - **Guide arrow (user's request):** the tracker's round `Guide` button (left of the badge; `OnColor` / `OffColor` / `OnIconColor` / `OffIconColor` / `OnShadowColor` / `OffShadowColor` attributes, an `IconShadow` under the icon) toggles `Client.Controllers.GuideController`, on by default. Held down, it drops onto its `GuideLip` and darkens by `PressDarken` (0.18), and only flips on release. It points at Dale whenever a step is Ready, at the **nearest living enemy** of the target kind on an Active kill step, and nothing otherwise (earn / level / give-without-the-coins hide the button). The arrow is a clone of `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/Effects/GuideArrow` (invisible flat part, arrow `Decal` on Top; **a Top-face decal's top edge is the part's +Z**, so the code faces −Z away from the target), floating flat at **chest height** (`Height` 0.5 above the HumanoidRootPart; it used to sit on the raycast ground and climbed onto roofs) `Distance` studs ahead of the player, creeping `Slide` studs every `SlideSeconds`, hidden within `HideWithin` (9) studs. A black copy of the decal (`GuideArrowShadow`) lies on the ground straight below, found by a ray down from the arrow (`ShadowReach` 8, `ShadowTransparency` 0.55, fainter the higher it floats). A Render Scheduler system at priority 60, client-only, parented to `Workspace.Runtime`.
  - Designs: https://claude.ai/artifact/M6s1p4LLoAErQGcy347KVt. Skins are in `art/quest_ui.html` → `art/quest/` (see the `roblox-ui-icons` skill).
- **Testing damage:** an admin sets their own multiplier on the admin panel's Damage card (the player's `DamageMultiplier` attribute, not saved; everyone starts each session at ×1). The old Studio-only `Balance.Melee.testDamageMultiplier` is gone.
- **Quest rewards are shown (2026-10-11):** hand-in coins / EXP use `CoinService.spill` / `ExpService.spill` at a spot 4 studs from Dale toward the player, with `afterTalk`: `LootController` holds the counters, waits until `InputLocked` clears (the conversation ends), then flings the coins / stars there and flies them to the HUD. A reward weapon also gets the Index NEW popup (`IndexService.showWeapon`, which announces even if already found).
- **Start-with test lists (Studio only, both empty now):** `Balance.Melee.testStartWeapons` (owned from spawn, the first selected; `MeleeService.stateOf`) and `Tools.testStartTools`. Fill one for a test and empty it again; the admin panel's Give / Give All usually does the job instead.
- **The Index has its own save** (user's request 2026-10-07): `IndexService` uses DataStore `Index`, key `u_<UserId>`, and merges on every save. Weapon drop chances there are multiplied by the killer's `LuckBoost`, capped at 100% (luck applies to every weapon, the Corn Cob too: a 20% drop at 5× luck is certain). **Since 2026-10-19 enemy material drops take luck too** (`MaterialService`; the Index slip shows them with it), **except secret cores**, which stay at their 1% (the user's rule).

Prices are placeholders the user can change; keep them equal to the website's.

Quest rewards and bought coins are never multiplied.

Admin sets ignore the per-world cap (clamped to 0..24). Playtested: Max all, Reset all, +1 and a typed Set; clean console.

Every word is `Admin.Text`; quick chips are `Admin.quick`. Gui attributes `StatusOk` / `StatusError` / `StatusSeconds`; tile attributes `Skin_<Rarity>`.

Card and row rims are the Inventory's teal (`0c7084`). The amount boxes are the Daily Rewards code box: an `AmountField` frame holding a white pill `Skin` and the borderless `Amount` TextBox centred on its face (code finds `Amount` by name at any depth). **Every button is a pill** (`Skin` 9-slice with `PillSlice`, the Inventory's `Label` clone on the face at y 54/136): give / add / level / heal / respawn / go green, take red, quick amounts / skip / reset gold, set teal; tabs teal, the open one gold (gui `SkinTabSelected` / `SkinTabIdle`); card actions take the template's `GiveSkin` / `RemoveSkin` / `StarterSkin` (grey). Cards are 4 a row (`CardSection` cell 0.23), mob and quest rows aspect 7.5. Every button grows ×1.06 from its centre (pinned in place; see "No button is placed by a UIListLayout"). The old panel is in `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-20/AdminPanelBefore`.
  - Aspect-sized grid cells and rows need a huge offset height to shrink from (`UDim2.new(0.315, 0, 0, 100000)`): an aspect constraint only shrinks, and a 0 height collapsed the tiles. Playtested end to end (F2, all three tabs, give, respawn, Close).

## UI is hand-built; code only wires it

**No hitches and nothing drawn in front of the player (2026-10-20, the user: switching weapons and killing enemies froze the game for half a second; panels, weapons and armour drew piece by piece the first time).**
- **Never rebuild a list on every attribute change.** `InventoryUI.renderGrid` used to destroy and re-clone ~70 shared tiles (each carrying its Drink / Sell / Confirm menus) on every weapon switch (`SelectedWeapon` / `Holstered` / `HeldTool`) and every drop (`Materials`, `Potions`, `OwnedWeapons`...), open or not: measured with a full bag 190–400 ms per change. Now each thing held keeps ONE tile for the session (`tiles[kind:id]`, its buttons wired once and reading the tile's state when clicked), a render refills only what changed (a fill is keyed on its pill text and `InfusedCores`), re-orders and hides the tiles of things not shown; renders are asked for with `renderGrid()` and run once at the end of the frame (`task.defer`). Measured after: under 1 ms per change, no frame over 30 ms through six weapon/tool switches and four kills (each kill was 90–110 ms). The admin panel's lists only re-render while the panel is open on that page (`live(page, render)` in `AdminUI`; opening renders the page). **A new list that follows attributes** reuses its items the same way or renders only while its panel is open.
- **`Client.UI.Preloader` (last in `UI_ORDER`) loads everything on join behind a cover:** the hand-built `StarterGui/ScreenFade` (`Black`, authored clear so Studio's Edit view isn't blacked out, goes opaque the moment the gui reaches the player; `Loading` "Loading..." label, Luckiest Guy; gui attributes `MaxSeconds` 20, `RevealSeconds` 0.5; DisplayOrder 200). It `PreloadAsync`s every ScreenGui in PlayerGui (closed panels too), `ReplicatedStorage/Assets`, every `rbxassetid://` in the `Shared.Config` modules (icons, portraits, world cards) and the armour models, waits for the download queue, waits for `DataLoaded`, switches every closed ScreenGui on for four frames with its `PopScale` at 1 (laid out and drawn once), then fades the cover. About 2.5 s in Studio. **Only image ids can be preloaded as strings** (a bare content string is fetched as an image: the 133 armour mesh ids failed that way), so `ArmorService.start` puts a plain copy of `ServerStorage/Assets/Armor` (no scripts or tags) in `ReplicatedStorage/Assets/ArmorModels` for it. `Preloader.whenDone(fn)` / `isDone()`: the World banner's first show waits for it. FadeController's teleport fades share the Black frame.

**Everything scales; nothing is fixed in pixels (user's rule, 2026-10-11: the game ships on phones too).**
- Sizes and positions are scale only (no Offset), with `UIAspectRatioConstraint` / `SizeConstraint` keeping shapes. Content of varying length is laid out by layouts, never placed by hand: `UIListLayout` (with `Wraps` for chip rows), `UIGridLayout`, `UIFlexItem` (`Fill`) and `AutomaticSize`. An aspect-sized list item starts tall (`UDim2.new(x, 0, 0, 100000)`) so the constraint can shrink it, and a ScrollingFrame's `CanvasSize` follows its layout's `AbsoluteContentSize` in code.
- **`Client.Util.Responsive`** (first in `UTIL_ORDER`) scales every pixel-only value by screen height / 1080: `UIStroke.Thickness`, `UITextSizeConstraint` Max/Min, `ScrollBarThickness`, 9-slice `SliceScale`, the Offset part of `UICorner` / `UIPadding` / `UIListLayout.Padding` / `UIGridLayout.CellPadding`, and `BillboardGui.Size`. The authored value is kept in a `Base<Property>` attribute (clones carry it). Covers PlayerGui and BillboardGuis in Workspace; SurfaceGuis (world signs) are left alone. Code that sets one of these itself uses `Responsive.px(n)` / `Responsive.setStroke(stroke, px)` (InventoryUI rims, RobuxShopUI `FaceTop` / `FaceBottom`). Text is TextScaled (with a cap) or sized in code from a box's height.
- Code may still place things in pixels when they come from live measurements (HudFly paths, canvas sizes, IndexUI's slip shift).
- **Quest card (Dialogue) is a bottom-up stack (user, 2026-10-11):** `Window/Body` (UIListLayout, bottom-aligned) holds `Text` (UIFlexItem Fill: takes what is left), `Objective`, `Reward` (AutomaticSize Y; `Caption` + `Chips`, a wrapping row whose chips keep their shape by aspect) and `Choices`. Buttons sit at the bottom, rewards right above them, the objective right above the rewards; an extra reward row pushes the objective up and the text box gives way.
- HUD side buttons are in `WeaponHud/SideButtons` (UIListLayout); code finds them with `FindFirstChild(name, true)`.
- Checked on a simulated 844 x 390 phone screen: proportions hold. Everything is physically small on a phone (it scales with screen height); a phone-size boost is a design decision still open.


The user wants every UI designed by hand in Studio, not generated by code. **Never build UI from scratch in a script** (no `Instance.new("Frame")` trees). Code may only show and hide, fill in live values, and clone hand-built templates for lists that come from data.

- **Buttons are chunky pills, and every clickable button grows on hover and squashes on a click (2026-10-20, the user's pick: style A on https://claude.ai/artifact/LqroedjkDQQnsskNtjbaDh).** Five 9-slice skins (`art/tools/ui/pill_buttons.py` → `art/ui/buttons/`): green `74554305270923` (buy, craft, claim, equip), teal `106172171376476`, gold `78197739773020` (filter, sell), red `121027922081384` (sell mode, cancel), grey `75275987375405` (locked, owned, done). They replaced every old button skin and the `Skin*` state attributes. **Both ends are perfect semicircles (the user, 2026-10-20: the first version's ends were too small, so the top and bottom looked like two pieces):** the image is a stadium (radius = half its 136 px height) whose lip is the face minus the face moved up, and its corners cover the whole height: ScaleType Slice, SliceCenter (68, 67, 332, 69) and a `PillSlice` attribute of 134, which makes `Util.Responsive` set SliceScale = the image's own AbsoluteSize.Y / 134 (on every size change) instead of the screen factor. The label (or the Weaponsmith's `Content` price group) is centred on the face, above the lip: the face runs from 8 to 136 − 36 source rows. The Robux Buy's `FaceTop` / `FaceBottom` are those source rows (8 / 36), turned into pixels from the skin's height. Locked Weaponsmith prices use the Buy's `LockedTextColor`.
  - `Client.Util.ButtonFx` (second in `UTIL_ORDER`) wires every GuiButton in PlayerGui that has a hand-built UIScale child named **`FxScale`** (on all 138 player buttons; the dialogue's click catchers and the world-sign Buys have none): hover grows it to `FxHover` (1.06), a press squashes it to `FxPress` (0.92) and it springs back. **Only clickable buttons react:** `Active` (and `Interactable`) true and no `Disabled` attribute. The UI modules set `Active = false` when a button can't be used: ShopUI (owned / too expensive), CraftingUI and UpgradesUI (the Locked look), RobuxShopUI (owned), DailyRewardsUI (claimed), InfuserUI. `FxNoHover` (buttons whose own code grows them: side buttons, hotbar slots) and `FxNoPress` turn off one half. **One UIScale per button:** Roblox applies only one UIScale per object, so a button with an `FxScale` must carry no other. The old hand-built `PressScale`s (59: every panel's X, the stall, admin and leaderboard tabs, the Robux Buys) were removed on 2026-10-20 with the code that dipped them (PanelWindow, AdminUI, RobuxShopUI); while both were there, those buttons showed no hover or press at all (the user's report). **No button is placed by a UIListLayout (2026-10-20, the user: "EVERY BUTTON ... needs to be expanding from the center").** In a list, Roblox applies a UIScale only along the list's direction, an item can't grow past the list's cross size, and a flex neighbour or an edge alignment moves its centre, so 187 buttons grew lopsided or not at all. Now: **fixed groups are pinned** (each piece centre-anchored at its measured spot in scale, the list, its UIPadding and the pieces' UIFlexItems removed): the admin header row, tab column, Player-page rows and cards, the WeaponCard / MaterialCard (+ `Buttons`) / MobRow / QuestRow templates, the Leaderboard stat tabs; **lists filled by code are UIGridLayouts** (a grid grows each item round its cell's middle): the Upgrades `List` (1 column, cell 0.9 wide, aspect 5.2), the Index `Worlds` (0.97, aspect 2.5645), the Crafter `Sets` (CraftingUI sets the cell from the frame's `Cell_Armor` (0.75, 0.44) / `Cell_Potions` (0.4, 0.23) attributes). The shared `Assets/UI/WeaponTile` and the Weaponsmith's art tiles are centre-anchored. A pinned piece with `SizeConstraint = RelativeYY` (the X, badges, portraits) keeps its width = its height in scale (a width measured against the parent's width collapsed them). **Check with the button audit** (`~/.claude/skills/roblox-ui-icons/references/button_audit.luau`, client datamodel in a playtest): it grows every visible button to 1.06 alone and lists any that didn't grow 1.06× on both axes round an unmoved centre; on 2026-10-20 all 560 passed (the Dialogue's two click catchers have no effect on purpose), and the HUD side buttons and hotbar (their own grow code) measured ×1.15 with the centre unmoved. A new button goes in a grid or is placed by hand, centre-anchored, never in a list. Check an effect by the button's drawn `AbsoluteSize`, never the UIScale's value: measured 2026-10-20, the Weaponsmith's Tools tab 253.8 → 269.1 wide hovered, 233.5 held, and stays grown once selected; its X 84.3 → 89.3 hovered, 77.5 held, closing only on release; the Inventory X 76.8 → 81.5. **Selected stays grown (2026-10-20, the user):** a `Selected` attribute keeps a button at `FxSelected` (default the hover size); the modules set it on the open tab (ShopUI, CraftingUI, AdminUI, LeaderboardUI tabs and periods), the chosen Upgrades row and the chosen Crafter set. **Everything grows from its centre** (the user: "like the Inventory, Index buttons"): free buttons are anchored at (0.5, 0.5) (30 re-anchored in place on 2026-10-20), a list holding buttons is centred on its cross axis (and on its main axis where its items fill it, e.g. the stall tabs), so a grown button pushes its neighbours aside; a grid grows each item round its cell. The Upgrades `List` rows are 0.9 wide, centred, with 8 px top / bottom padding and the scroll bar inset, so a grown row isn't cut off. The Daily Rewards `CodeBox/Input` is a white pill (`74974380783926`, the same PillSlice setup). **A new button needs an `FxScale` and a centre anchor**, code that greys a button out sets its `Active`, and code that marks a choice sets `Selected`.
- Screens are ScreenGuis in `StarterGui`: `WeaponsShop`, `CraftingShop`, `Inventory`, `Index`, `RobuxShop` (each has a `Panel` attribute; `PanelWindow` shows one at a time and wires any button named `Close`), `WeaponHud`, `IndexPopup`, `Dialogue` (NPC talk, pops itself via its own `PopScale`), `QuestTracker`. All have `ResetOnSpawn` off.
- **An open panel is the only thing that can be clicked (2026-10-22, the user: buttons could be clicked through a panel; "the only way out is walking away (if it is a station), clicking outside of the panel, or clicking the X").** `PanelWindow` hides WeaponHud's `SideButtons`, `Hotbar` and `Attack` and the whole `QuestTracker` while any panel is open (saving what each was, so nothing another module hid comes back), and brings them back when it closes; the player card and coins stay. Every panel ScreenGui has a hand-built **`Catcher`** (a full-screen invisible TextButton, ZIndex 0, under the Window): a click outside the window closes the panel. **A frame doesn't stop a button behind it getting the click** (an Active Frame only stops the world, e.g. a weapon swing; measured: a click on the Inventory's hint text reached the Catcher underneath), so every `Window` has an invisible **`Blocker`** TextButton at the back of it (its lowest ZIndex - 1), and so does any piece that sticks out of the window (the Index's `DropSlip`). Window pieces are Active too. Neither has an `FxScale` (no hover, no sound). Playtested: two clicks on the Inventory left it open with no swing; a click where the hidden Index button sits closed the Inventory without opening the Index; the Crafter's ring opens and walking away closes it, the X closes; the HUD comes back each time. **A new panel needs its own `Catcher` and `Blocker`.**
- **Panels pop open and shut:** each panel's `Window` has a `UIScale` named `PopScale`. `PanelWindow` grows it from 0 with an overshoot over the panel's `OpenSeconds`, and shrinks it to 0 over `CloseSeconds` before turning the panel off. A new panel needs a centre-anchored `Window` with its own `PopScale`.
- In-world templates are in `ReplicatedStorage.Assets.UI`: `EnemyHealthBar`, `DamagePopup`. List templates live in a `Templates` folder inside their ScreenGui (`Inventory/Templates/WeaponCard`) and are kept `Visible = false`.
- Styling that changes with state (equipped/locked/disabled colours, end-screen wording, popup colours) lives in **attributes on the template**, so the user can restyle without code.
- Code finds pieces **by name**, so keep names stable when editing, and tell the user which names the code depends on.
- Sizes are in scale, with `UIAspectRatioConstraint` / `SizeConstraint = RelativeYY` and `UITextSizeConstraint` on text. That way the editor preview matches the game on any screen.
- **Index** (`StarterGui/Index`, `Panel` = "Index", layout C "Open book") is wired by `Client.UI.IndexUI`.
  - The book `Window` holds `LeftPage` (`Title`, `Progress`, `AllButton`, `Worlds`), `RightPage` (`Heading`, `Grid`, `Empty`), `Close`, `Ribbon`, and `DropSlip` (`Portrait`, `EnemyName`, `Kills`, `Rows` (a ScrollingFrame since 2026-10-11; IndexUI sets its CanvasSize from the UIListLayout's AbsoluteContentSize, because AutomaticCanvasSize ignores aspect-ratio rows), `NoDrops`) to the left of the book.
  - `Templates`:
    - `WorldCard`: the world's `profile` image, with `Rim`, `WorldName` and `Count`.
    - `LockedWorld`: used when a world has no enemies yet.
    - `EnemyCard`: skins, colours, ??? wording and `Tilt` are attributes.
    - `DropRow`: `Tile` with an `Icon` viewport, and `Skin_<Rarity>` attributes.
  - Silhouettes are `ImageColor3` tinted to `SilhouetteColor`.
  - The HUD button is `WeaponHud/IndexButton`.
- **The Index covers the whole game (2026-10-20, the user: "cover almost everything"; tab strip B and Title 2 on https://claude.ai/artifact/25K21YcFmckdyVGPEa3VpX).** Eight sections per world (`Config/Index` `Sections`): Creatures, Weapons, Armour, Tools, Materials (enemy drops, then gathered), Fish, Potions (Tier I on its own world, Tier II / III on the first world of their band, only those with a recipe) and Treasures (the quest items). **Cores and secret enemies are never in it.** `Index.entries(world, section)`, `progress(world, progressOf(player))`, `sectionOf(id)`, `itemDef(id)`.
  - **Counting (`IndexService`):** every rise of the `Materials`, `Potions` and `OwnedTools` attributes after `DataLoaded` adds to the player's count (what they held on join is found quietly), saved in the Index store as `items` and published as `IndexItems` ("OakLog=128,WoodenAxe=1"). The first tool, fish, potion, treasure or Rare-or-better material shows the NEW popup (kind `Item`); common materials only their toast. Admin Give All calls `IndexService.hush(player, 2)` so it counts without a popup per item.
  - **Titles (Title 2):** a world with every page found earns its **Collector** title ("Meadows Collector", "Redwood Collector", "Caverns Collector"; `Index.TitleNames`, placeholders); that plus the world's core infused into the Worldheart Blade (`Index.coreDone`) earns its **Completionist** title ("World 1 Completionist"...). Earned titles are kept (saved `titles`) even if a world gains entries later. The first earn plays the popup (kind `Title`, the Popup's `TitleIcon` / `TitleSecretIcon` trophies, `TitleNewText` "NEW TITLE!"). One is worn at a time (`Remotes.EquipTitle(id)`, "" takes it off; saved `title`), published as `Titles` / `Title`; `ChatTagController` puts it first in chat ([Meadows Collector] aqua 8FE0EC, [World 1 Completionist] gold FFD23F).
  - **The book (hand-built, wired by `IndexUI`):** left page `Progress` ("78 of 86 found", its `Format`) and `ProgressBar/Fill`; each `Templates/WorldCard` has a `Percent` chip (the old three count chips are gone). Right page: `Heading`, `Total` (right of the ribbon), `Titles` (`Collector` and `Secret` cards: `Skin` with `Rim`, `Icon`, `TitleName`, `Note`; the Secret card shows only once the Collector is earned, as "???" under a red wax seal until the Completionist is; colours and words are the Titles frame's attributes; an earned card toggles wearing it and the worn one is `Selected`), `Tabs` (8 pinned pill buttons `Tab<Section>`: `Skin`, `Icon`, `Label`, `Count`; attribute `Tab`; the open one gold, `Selected`), then `Grid`. Creatures, weapons and armour keep their photo / tile rows (no section headers now: the tab names it); the other five fill each `Section`'s new `Entries` list with `Templates/EntryRow` rows (`Tile`, `ItemName`, `RarityPill`, `Chip1` / `Chip2`, `CountCaption` / `CountValue`; every word an attribute): a tool's kind, tier and how you get it; a material's source and drop chance (with luck) or the tool a node needs; a fish's spot and bite; a potion's effect; a treasure's quest; and how many you've caught / found / collected. Art: `art/tools/icons/index_titles.html` → `art/ui/index/title_collector.png` (`rbxassetid://122026585023962`), `title_completionist.png` (`140529021420881`), `title_seal.png` (`131830523209349`). The old Index is in `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-20/IndexBefore`.
  - Playtested: admin gives counted (Minnow 3 + 2 → 5), popups for the fish, tool, potion and an Epic material but not Oak Log; Give All quiet; the four World 1 creatures killed → Meadows Collector popup, worn from the book; the Farm Core infused at the World 1 Infuser → World 1 Completionist earned and worn; clean console.
- **NEW popups** (`StarterGui/IndexPopup/Popup`, ray-burst style) are played by `Client.UI.IndexPopup` from `Remotes.IndexDiscovered`, one at a time. Enemies use the `*Enemy` attributes (teal). **Weapon popups scale with rarity** (user's request 2026-10-09: "all of the pop up colours being green is lame"): every `<Name>_<Rarity>` attribute on `Popup` sets that tier's look: `Banner`, `Rays` (image, "" = none), `RaysColor`, `RaysTransparency`, `RaysSpin`, `SparklesSpin` / `SparklesColor`, `GlowColor`, `GlowPulse`, `NameStroke`, `ChanceColor`, `PopFrom` (>1 = slams down from that size), `PopSeconds`, `ShowSeconds`, `Flash` / `FlashColor`, `Dim`, `Rainbow`, `NewText`. Common: plain grey, no rays. Uncommon / Rare: green / blue rays. Epic: purple, sparkles, a breathing glow, a faint flash. Legendary: gold banner with shine stripes, sparkles, a gold flash and a light dim. Secret: slams in at 2.6× over a dimmed screen with a white flash, rainbow rays and hue-cycling glow, sparkles and name outline, and the tag reads "SECRET!". Hand-built pieces: `Popup/Sparkles`, `Popup/Picture` (the weapon's drawn icon; `Icon` viewport is the fallback), and the full-screen `IndexPopup/Dim` and `Flash` frames. Art: `art/rarity_popup.html` → `art/popup/` (banners common `91383673106322`, rare `76928168410683`, epic `89709582915826`, legendary `116001337083129`, secret `128195849513219`; uncommon keeps the old green `89942307214175`; rainbow rays `126242731276012`; sparkles `78047475344560`). The Weathervane Spear and Pumpkin King's Mace (2026-10-11) are the first Legendaries. `Templates/Chip` holds the drop chances.
- **Gathered material icons (2026-10-19):** drawn in the craft-icons style (`art/tools/icons/w1_gather_icons.html` → `svg_render.py` → `finish_w1_gather.py` → `art/icons/materials/`): Oak Log `104043389166635`, Stone `118574059729672`, Minnow `135916252607286` (slim, silver-blue), Sunfish `101784354405776` (deep, teal with an orange belly and red ear spot), Golden Carp `105639157556378` (gold scales and a sparkle; its barbel was removed, user: it read as a tongue). The three fish share one shape, like the one FishShadow model.
- Index portraits (`portrait` on enemy defs) and world pictures (`profile` on world defs) are uploaded images, with sources in `art/portraits/enemies/` and `art/portraits/worlds/` (World 3's card `125541783517148`, 2026-10-17). **Secret enemies have no `portrait`**, because the Index, NEW popup and quest UI read it. Their picture is `adminPortrait`, which only the admin panel's Mobs tab shows (Corn Wraith, Fairy, Gem Mole, 2026-10-17). A new enemy needs a portrait rendered the same way: turned slightly right, seen slightly from above, on a green screen and chroma-keyed.
- **Weaponsmith Tools tab (2026-10-19, user's request):** `WeaponsShop/Window/Tabs` (in the header between the world pill and the coins) holds `TabWeapons` / `TabTools` (TextButtons with a `Tab` attribute and a `Label`; gui attributes `TabSelectedFill` / `TabSelectedText` / `TabIdleFill` / `TabIdleText`). Rows with `Kind = "Tool"` (`Item_WoodenAxe` / `Item_WoodenPickaxe` / `Item_WoodenRod`, copies of a weapon row with the combat chips swapped for a `Desc` line) show only on the Tools tab; ShopUI reads `Config/Tools` (price, world, `OwnedTools`) for them and buys through `Remotes.BuyTool` (StallService: owned / coins / at that world's Weapons stall, then `ToolService.grant` and a save). `Tools.testStartTools` is now empty, so tools must be bought (the admin `tool` action still grants one).
- **Inventory Tools filter (2026-10-19):** the Filter dropdown has `Tools` (`TopBar/Options/Tools`, between Weapons and Armor; the options were re-spaced to fit five). Owned tools show as WeaponTiles with a "Tier 1" pill, **display only** (user, 2026-10-19: tools can't be equipped or put away there; they come out of their hotbar slot, which always holds the best of its kind). Gaining a tool calls `MeleeService.refreshTool`, so a better tool of the kind in hand replaces it at once. The Hint's `Text_Tools` is "Your best tools go in the hotbar." **The grid scrolls to the bottom (fixed 2026-10-19):** `AutomaticCanvasSize` is off and InventoryUI sizes the canvas from the UIGridLayout (`fitCanvas`); the gap above the first row and below the last matches the side gap (user, 2026-10-19), measured from the leftmost tile; inside a ScrollingFrame a UIPadding's scale is a share of the canvas, so `fitCanvas` sets canvas = content + 2 × gap and the top / bottom padding to gap / canvas.
- Shop stall items are hand-placed cards with `ItemId` / `Price` attributes, bought with coins. The Weapons shop (`WeaponsShop/Window/List/Item_<id>` rows) is wired by `Client.UI.ShopUI`: it fills each `Art/Icon` viewport and `Coins/Amount`, and styles each `Buy` button from its `SkinBuy` / `SkinLocked` / `SkinOwned` attributes.
- **Stall signs (2026-10-20, the user: the HUD's font, no highlight), in all three worlds (the World 2 and 3 Crafting / Weapons stalls too):** each stall's `Roof/SignBoard` (an invisible 6.2 × 1.45 sheet, 0.02 thick, lying 0.02 off the gable; the user: no layer between the wall and the UI; the `SignBack` slab is gone, old parts in `MapBackup_2026-10-20/SignPartsBefore`; its bottom sits 0.25 above the gable's `BeamFront`, which stands 0.14-0.19 proud and hid the plate's bottom from the ground once the sign lay on the wall: checked from a player's eye in front, no edge hidden, both top corners on the gable) carries a hand-built `SignText` SurfaceGui (Brightness 1, LightInfluence 0: no glow): `Plate` (the stall's ring colour shaded top to bottom by a UIGradient from the gui's `Top` / `Bottom` attributes, rounded, 4 px ink rim, a faint `Shine` band) holding `Label` (Luckiest Guy, white, 3 px ink stroke) over `LabelShadow` (ink, 0.07 lower). Crafting green 4fcf5c / 2c9a3e, Weapons red ff6464 / cc3d3d, Upgrades blue 3f8ae8 / 2558b8, Daily yellow ffd23f / e6a417. **The Crafting stand is green** (2026-10-20, the user; it had the Weaponsmith's reds): boards 3fa34d, stripes 2f8a3c, trim 1f5e2a (backups `CraftingStationBeforeRecolour`, `DailyStationBeforeRecolour` in `MapBackup_2026-10-20`). The old FredokaOne signs are in `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-20/SignText_<Station>`.
- Stall rings are hand-built parts (`Merchants/<Stall>/ShopRing`) tagged `Interactable`, with a `Station` attribute and a SurfaceGui holding `Idle` and `Inside` looks.
- Studio's `screen_capture` may not show BillboardGuis (enemy bars, damage numbers), and in a playtest it doesn't always follow a scripted camera. Verify those with property checks.

## Map and assets

- Map contract is in `Config/MapLayout`: `Workspace.Worlds.<W##_Name>/` with `Ground`, `Structures`, `Decorations`, `Spawns/EnemySpawns`, `Spawns/BossSpawns`, `Enemies`, `Bosses`, `Chests`, `Ambience`.
- Enemy templates: `ServerStorage.Assets.Enemies` / `.Bosses`. Weapon models: `ReplicatedStorage.Assets.Weapons`.
- Visual style: blocky, flat colours, mostly `SmoothPlastic`. Forward is **-Z** for every model.
- **Parts or meshes (agreed 2026-10-07):**
  - **Studio parts** for the map's base (ground, paths, hedges, mounds, waterfall, gates), building shells, and anything players stand on.
  - **Blender meshes** for decorations, props, building dressing, weapons, enemy pieces (one mesh per moving piece, still rigged with PartRig) and pickups.
  - Meshes stay low-poly and flat-shaded, with one MeshPart per colour and no textures, coloured in Studio.
  - The user imports each FBX from `art/meshes/` with File → Import 3D; I do the rest (see the `blender-modeling` skill).
  - First meshes: `Assets/Effects/Coin` and `ExpStar`, each with a star symbol.
  - World 1's flowers and grass tufts are mesh clusters cloned from `ServerStorage.Assets.Decorations`:
    - `Tulips_N` / `Daisies_N`, where N is 1/3/5/7 flowers, coloured per cluster;
    - `GrassTuft_A/B/C`.

    World 1 is down to 4,638 parts.
  - Bushes are mesh too, also cloned from `Assets.Decorations`: `Bush_<Plain|Flowering|Berry|Apple>_<A|B|C>`, 1–3 MeshParts each.
    - **Apple bushes** are new (user's request): 12 of the plain bushes nearest the farm and apple trees became apple bushes.
    - World 1 is now 4,217 parts.
  - Trees are mesh too: `Assets.Decorations/Tree_<Pine|Oak|Birch|Apple|Blossom>_<A|B>`, 2–3 MeshParts each.
    - The `Trunk` keeps collision (`CanCollide`, Hull fidelity); leaves, apples and petals are walk-through.
    - World 1 is now 3,865 parts (it was 8,213 before any optimisation).
  - Rocks are mesh too: `Assets.Decorations/Rock_<Boulder|Mossy>_<A|B>` and `Rock_<Cliff|Standing|Pebbles>_A`, 2–3 MeshParts each.
    - Every stone has bevelled edges and cut corners.
    - Rock pieces are solid; moss and pebbles are walk-through.
    - World 1 is now 3,820 parts.
  - Fallen logs are mesh too: `Assets.Decorations/Log_<A|B>`, 5 MeshParts each (`Bark`, `Ends`, `Moss`, `Caps`, `Stalks`, where `Stalks` includes the cap spots).
    - Log A has 2 mushrooms and log B has 3. Only `Bark` is solid.
    - World 1 is now 3,779 parts.
  - Ledge fences are mesh too: `Assets.Decorations/Fence_A` (`Posts`, `Rails`; solid, Box collision).
    - Each keeps the old fence's exact pivot, with its diagonal brace turned toward the nearest path.
    - World 1 is now 3,757 parts.
  - The farm props (`Decorations/Farm/<HayYard|BarnYard|CottageYard|CropField>`) are one MeshPart per colour per area, placed directly in each area model. The `Soil` rows in the crop field stay parts.
    - The front crop row is **carrots** (user's request).
    - Solid pieces use `PreciseConvexDecomposition`, because one mesh holds several props: hay, barrels, crates, trough, scarecrow pole and shirt, field fence. Crops, straw, hat and bands are walk-through.
    - World 1 is now 3,643 parts.
  - Pond plants are mesh too: `Assets.Decorations/Reeds_<A|B|C>` (`Stems`, `Heads`) and `LilyPad_A` (plain `Pad`) / `LilyPad_B` (`Pad`, `Bloom`, `Centre`; a water lily). All walk-through.
    - **Every World 1 decoration group is now mesh.** World 1 is at 3,510 parts, down from 8,213.
    -
  - **Building details (2026-10-07):** each is rebuilt in place, one mesh per colour, inside the building model.
    - The flower beds are in `Fountain/Details` and `House/Details`: petal meshes per colour, plus `Centres`, `Stems`, `Leaves`, and the fountain's `Coins`.
    - The windmill sails are the `Windmill/Sails` model (`Wood`, `Cloth`, `Axle`), tagged **`Spinner`**. It sits 1.5 studs above the old hub, so the hub clears the top window.
    - The bed stems run up into each bloom (fixed 2026-10-07; the old part stems stopped short).
    - The cottage window boxes are meshes directly in `Cottage`: `Box`, `Soil`, `Leaves`, `Red`/`Yellow`/`Pink` petals and yellow `Centres` (user's request).
    - The silo ladder (`Farmstead/Silo/Ladder`) and the barn door X trim (`Farmstead/Barn/Shell/Trim`) are meshes too.
    - World 1 is now 2,722 parts.
  - **Lamp posts (2026-10-07)** are mesh too: `Assets.Decorations/Lamp_A` (`Base`, `Wood`, `Iron`, Neon `Glass` holding the `PointLight`), cloned into `Ambience/Lamps` at each old pivot. The lantern hangs 2.1 studs out along the pivot's +Z.
    - Only `Wood` is solid (`PreciseConvexDecomposition`).
    - World 1 is now 2,618 parts.
- **Lighting (first pass 2026-10-07):** Roblox removed `Lighting.Technology`. It is now `LightingStyle` (Soft, kept for the cartoon look) plus `PrioritizeLightingQuality`, and scripts can set both.
  - Sun and ambient: `ClockTime` 14.4, `Brightness` 2.4, `ExposureCompensation` −0.25, a warm `ColorShift_Top`, a blue-grey `OutdoorAmbient` for tinted shade, and `EnvironmentDiffuseScale` 0.45.
  - Lighting children: `Atmosphere` (density 0.3, light haze), `Bloom` (threshold 1.6, so mostly Neon glows), `ColorCorrection` (+saturation, +contrast) and faint `SunRays`. There's also a `Sky` and `Terrain.Clouds`.
  - Later clock times put the plaza in the cliff's shadow.
  - The Lighting settings apply to every world; per-world presets will be needed for W02/W03.
  - **Per-world lighting (2026-10-21, the user's picks from screenshot sheets):** World 1 "Golden afternoon" (ClockTime 16.2, warm sun and haze, a little more saturation, faint sun rays), World 2 "Misty morning" (ClockTime 9.6, blue-grey haze, Atmosphere density 0.46, no sun rays: World 2's rule). Each is a world def's `lighting` in `Config/Worlds` (Lighting properties plus `Atmosphere` / `ColorCorrection` / `SunRays` tables; `WorldTypes.WorldDef.lighting`). `Client.Controllers.AmbienceController` eases to the look of the player's `CurrentWorld` over 4 s and still lets an `AmbienceZone` (the stump room's twilight, World 3's `CaveAmbience`) override ClockTime / Ambient / OutdoorAmbient / Brightness on top; a world without `lighting` gets the lighting as built in Studio, which is World 1's golden look. **World 3 "Deep sapphire" (the user's pick, 2026-10-24, from four looks shot in three halls):** ClockTime 14, Brightness 2, ExposureCompensation -0.1, Ambient and OutdoorAmbient 7d7fb8, ColorShift_Top e6e8ff, Atmosphere density 0.38 (colour 6f78c8, decay 2a2d66, haze 1.6, no glare), ColorCorrection contrast 0.18, saturation 0.22, tint d6dcff, no sun rays. Its `CaveAmbience_Cave` zone sat past the cave's south end (so the cave had shown World 1's golden look) and was removed; `CaveAmbience_WayDown` (over the cave mouth and the way down) now overrides Ambient / OutdoorAmbient with the same 7d7fb8, so walking down from World 2 blends into it. Playtested: the cave reads every value, World 2's look comes back on leaving. Playtested: World 1 16.2 / density 0.32, World 2 9.6 / 0.46, the stump room 18.2 and back; clean console.
- **Ambient sound (2026-10-07):** no code; looping Sounds with `Playing = true` start for every player on join. They live in `W01_SunnyMeadows/Ambience/Sounds`:
  - Global beds, as Sounds directly in the folder: `Birds` (ProSoundEffects "Birds Park 1") and `Music` (APM "Polska från Flackarp (b)", a rustic violin folk tune, until 2026-10-21; now "Tranquil Time", see below).
  - Positional water, as Sounds inside invisible anchor parts: `Stream` at the stream steps into the pond, and `Fountain`.
  - Volumes: each Sound uses a `SoundService` SoundGroup, `Ambience` (0.7) or `Music` (0.4). Balance the mix there.
  - Alternatives to audition are in `ServerStorage.AudioCandidates_2026-10-07`. To swap one in, copy its `SoundId`.
  - A third positional sound, `Waterfall`, sits at the falls.
  - **Each world's beds play only in that world (2026-10-21):** a Sound directly in a world's `Ambience/Sounds` is a bed (World 1 `Birds` + `Music`, World 2 `Birds` = PSE "Sonoma Morning Birds" `9119383684` with a woodpecker, `Wind` = PSE "Farmland Presence 2" `9112777914`, both the user's picks); the server plays them all and `Client.Controllers.WorldAudioController` turns this player's copies up to their `BaseVolume` attribute in the `CurrentWorld` and to 0 elsewhere (2.5 s fades). Sounds inside parts (water) stay positional. Before this, World 1's birds and music played everywhere. **The enemy death sound stays the one cartoon pop for every enemy** (the user, 2026-10-21: no per-creature cries). **Music re-picked, tamer (the user, 2026-10-21):** World 1 `Music` is APM "Tranquil Time (a)" `1840970401` (volume 0.5; it replaced "Polska fran Flackarp", the old violin folk tune), World 2's new `Music` APM "Twilight Blue" `1842361805`, a solo acoustic guitar (volume 0.65: its master plays about 40% quieter, measured with `PlaybackLoudness` in a playtest, which reads 0 in Edit mode). The user's backups are in `ServerStorage/MusicBackups_2026-10-21`: World 1 "Childhood Song" `1839273044` and "Dawn 2" `1838792473` (plus the old Polska), World 2 "Glisten" `9048137241` and "Peaceful Grace" `9042152099`. Playtested: World 1 plays only its music, World 2 only its own.
  - The blue wall at the far end is `Shared.Gates.World2Gate.Barrier`, not water.
- **World 2 gate, style B "Ranger's Lodge Gate" (2026-10-10, user's pick on https://claude.ai/artifact/72mLsePFyuCMFgXfafDYid; World 2 is a forest).** The model is `Workspace.Shared.Gates.World2Gate`, between the rim cliffs at z −287. The user approved it, and the old stone gate's backup was deleted on 2026-10-10.
  - **Parts:** `Post_L` / `Post_R` (log cylinders d 7, 2 studs into the cliffs, tops above the beam at y 39) with `PostEnd_*` discs. `Barrier` is the green see-through vine curtain (24.4 wide, from the ground to local 29.6, so its top runs up into the arch beam everywhere; user's request). `SignBoard` is invisible and carries the sign; its bottom is 7 above the ground, so players walk under it. `Rope_L/R` hang it from the beam.
  - **Blender details** (`gate_build` text block, collection `Gate`, `art/meshes/gate_world2.fbx` + `gate_world2_offsets.json`, placed at the gate pivot (0, 8, −287) × offset): `Beam` (curved, carved `Carving` dashes), `Leaves` / `LeavesLight` garland, `Flowers` / `FlowerCentres`, `Vines` (bands round the posts), `Bark` grooves, `EndRings`, `LanternFrame` / Neon `LanternGlass`, and `CurtainVines` / `CurtainLeaves`, which open with the curtain. Placed 2026-10-10: `Beam` is solid (Hull), the rest walk-through; `LanternGlass` is Neon with a PointLight in the middle of each lantern's glass (`Light` attachments). The lanterns hang at x ±8.1, the widest gap between the curtain vines (0.29 clear of the nearest leaf). Each is one connected piece (user's report, 2026-10-10): base, four posts running into the base and a rim, glass filling the frame, a roof on the rim, and a knob with the hanger rod going up into the beam. The sign's `Rope_L/R` run down behind the sign, past its drawn top edge. `Bushes` holds two `Assets.Decorations` bush clones at the posts' feet.
  - **Config/Gates** holds the gates (`World2`: world 2, level 25 (the user's pick, 2026-10-19; 10 on the old curve), 2,500 coins since 2026-10-19's World 1 balance) and **every word the sign shows** (`Gates.Text`, the user's rule: UI text lives in code). The title is the world's `name` in `Config/Worlds` (W02 is "The Redwood Forest").
  - **Sign:** `StarterGui/GateSign_World2`, a hand-built SurfaceGui (`GateId` = "World2", Back face, 30 px per stud = 360 × 340). Its pieces are `Banner/Label`, `Title`, `LevelRow` / `CoinsRow` / `MaterialRow1` / `MaterialRow2` (each with a `*Shadow` copy; `Icon`, `Label`, `Check`, `Have`; a gate's `materials` fill the material rows, set 2026-10-19 to 20 Oak Log + 20 Stone for World 2, and rows past its list hide), and `Buy` (`Content`: `Label`, `Coin`, `Amount`; `PressScale`) with `BuyLip`. State colours are `FillTop_` / `FillBottom_` / `Lip_` / `Ink_<Met|Locked|Unlocked>` and `ShortColor` attributes on the gui. The `SignBoard` part is invisible, so only the rounded sign shows. The template's text is realistic placeholder ("World 2", "Buy" 5,000), and its UIPaddings hold the FontDrop correction, so the sign is centred in Edit mode too. Measured: the banner within 1 px, Buy within 2.
  - **Buying:** `Client.UI.GateUI` fills the sign from Level / Coins / HighestWorld and fires `Remotes.GateBuy { id }` when both requirements are met. `GateService` re-checks everything (within `BUY_RANGE` 40 of the board), spends the coins, calls `PlayerDataService.unlockWorld` and saves. Shapes are wobbly squashed rings round each site; the bottom step stays 2+ studs from the path, ruins and gate approaches. **Hills run under the hedges** (fixed 2026-10-11, user's report): the height field covers grass cells *and* cells under `Rim` blocks, so a hill meets a hedge as ground rising against it. **Cell membership is inclusive** (`>= lo − ε`, `<= hi + ε`): a strict test missed every cell whose centre sat on the seam between two merged grass blocks, which cut 252 one-cell holes in rows through the hills ("cutoffs going through the hills"). Clashes are found with `GetPartsInPart` on every piece, with **CanQuery switched on for every part, including each fresh clone's pieces** (the templates' needles have it off, and missing that once let new trees overlap). The trees with the most clashes are removed first, then the gaps are refilled under the same test. Verified 0 contacts.
    - **On hills:** a tree's pivot sits on the step under it, and its footprint (9 rays round the trunk, radius 2.6 × scale) must all hit one height. A hill step only counts as cutting into a tree if its top is above the tree's base (every trunk reaches 0.1 into its own ground).
    - A **draped log** is tilted to the height difference between its ends (≥ 2), then lowered in 0.1 steps until its bark touches the steps, and backed off one step.
    - Trees: spots nearest the hedges first, the four kinds in turn, random turn, scale 0.9–1.1, then 10 more spaced out on the open meadow.

New markers (copies of `_01`, same height above ground, random turn): one more bull per lane (`Bull_04` west (−105, −52), `Bull_05` east (105, −40), `Bull_06` centre (−12, −88)), two more chickens per lane in the lower half (`Chicken_13`..`18`), and `Stallion_03` / `_04` at the junction's two north corners (−21, −170) / (20, −162), where the left, right and centre paths meet (the first two hold the south corners). Each spot passed the replant clearances (grass only, clear of paths, water, buildings and gathering nodes), its wander disc is free of solid decorations, and it is 16+ studs from any other marker.
- **Enemies roam beside the paths, never on them (2026-10-20, the user, Worlds 1 and 2):** every regular enemy's marker stands so its whole roaming area stays **1–2 studs off the nearest path** (the paths, ramp `Step`s and bridges; **not the hub or the boss arena**, the user's correction: those only need 1+ clear), measured from the wander disc's edge: `WanderRadius` + half the `Root`'s wider side. Bosses and secret enemies (Corn Wraith, Fairy: their rooms have no paths) are left alone. `ServerStorage.DevTools.SpawnBeside` does it (`S.run(world, { pathFolders, avoidFolders?, pathName?, plantable, skip?, only? })` → report, failed; `avoidFolders` = the hub / arena, kept 1+ off; `only` = a set of marker names): rings out from each marker's old spot (up to 70 studs) for the nearest spot whose gap is 1–2, the whole disc on one level of plantable grass, nothing solid in it (lamps count) and 16+ studs from other markers, then faces the marker at the nearest path point and keeps its height above the ground. World 1: 27 moved by the tool (`Chicken_05`, which had been set against the hub, re-run against the path: (−13.5, 115), beside the middle path); `Stallion_04` went to (14, −164) by hand after the standing rock in its way moved to (14.1, −147.8) (another `Rock_Standing_A` was moved by mistake on the first try and now stands at (25.3, −173.4), a valid spot). Measured: gaps 1.00–1.96, nearest neighbour median 40 studs. **World 2 spread out like World 1 (the user: World 2 looked cluttered; median nearest neighbour was 20.5 studs, World 1's 40):** planned off the map (a 1-stud ray grid of the ground, the trees and logs, dumped out of Studio) by an annealing search that maximises the smallest gap between enemies, then the average, over every spot whose disc lies on one level of grass, clear of paths, water, hedges, buildings, logs, rocks, the camp and the fairy ring: 6 foxes alternating sides of the first path; 6 foxes and 3 boars per lane with up to 2 of them in the middle of the loop (gap free there: `Fox_12` stands 3.0 off); **4 bears on the last path, 2 inside its bend (towards the arena) and 2 outside** (the user: 2 on each side), allowed up to 4 off the path (2.8–3.9). Result: nearest neighbour 31–52 studs (median 35, was 20.5), 24 of 28 at a 1–2 gap, all on level grass with nothing solid in the disc. 13 spruces that stood in the new roaming areas were parked in `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-20/W2TreesMovedForSpawns` and replanted elsewhere with `W2Dress` (trees still 137, the same mix). Playtested: every body on the ground, clean console. The simulations on the new spots: World 1 31.4 min typical, World 2 46.4.

- **Path and arena details (2026-10-08, user's request):**
  - `Ground/PathDetails/Trims`: 96 skinny edging strips (0.35 wide, 0.12 proud, 9c7a4e) along every `Ground/Paths` edge that meets grass or a drop. None where a path meets another path, the plaza, a bridge, the arena or a ramp `Step` within 1.1 studs of height.
    - **Rebuilt 2026-10-10 with `ServerStorage.DevTools.PathTrims`** (a dev tool: `loadstring(module.Source)()`, then `.build(opts)`; the old strips are in `ServerStorage.TrimsBackup_2026-10-10`). The user's rules: a strip lies **entirely on its own path block** and **sits on its top** (0.12 tall, nothing below the path top), so it never pokes into the ground or shows in a cliff face. **It never intersects anything.** **Corners (the user's call):** a path's **outer** corners have the normal joined outline; the Z-side strip runs to the corner and the X-side strip stops against it. **Inner** corners, where the path bends round a corner of grass and two blocks' strips meet, keep a small gap where the strips touch only at their corners. Strips are cut around bridges, fences, flowers and stones (all parts are made queryable while scanning), and collinear strips are joined. **Bug fixed 2026-10-10:** the edge test's downward rays used to hit strips laid earlier in the run (and World 1's stepping stones), read them as "not path", and stretched strips half a stud into inner corners. New strips now wait in a holding folder outside the rays' reach, and the rays include only the ground folders, never `PathDetails`. Checked: 0 inner corners joined in both worlds. Checked: 0 overlaps and 0 corners off a path in both worlds.
    - The edges were found by sampling each path rect's sides every 0.5 studs and raycasting just inside and just outside.
  - `Ground/PathDetails/SteppingStones`: 136 darker tan stones (bf9a66 ± shade), about one per 45 sq studs, each fully on bare path.

    - `ArenaFloor` is now the dark rim (45464c); a `Field` (6e7076) is inset 2 studs.
    - Rings and spokes: purple `OuterRing`, 4 dark `Spoke` bars (8 spokes), orange `Stud`s where the spokes meet the ring, orange `InnerRing`.

- **World 1 balance (2026-10-19, the user's target): about 30 minutes for a typical player** from spawning to buying the World 2 gate, with every weapon except the secret (drops, buys, Dale's spear, the Pumpkin Mace), both armours, Dale's 10 steps and the tools. Game passes, boosts, luck, potions (drinking and brewing), the Corn Cob, the Farm Core and the Corn Wraith are left out (the user's rule). Later worlds get longer, about 45 min, 1 h, 1.5 h, 2 h, 2.5 h, 3 h (memory `world-length-plan`). The numbers: kill coins Chicken 6–12, Bull 24–40, Stallion 40–60, King 90–140; the gate 2,500; Horsehair 50% and Harvest Knight needs 8; stall prices Sickle 150, Machete 400, Pitchfork 650, Shovel 700, Scythe 1,800; the Mace 5% (the user's pick, 2026-10-19, the top of the 1–5% rule) and the Corn Cob 0.1% (rare drop rules: a boss Legendary 1–5%, a secret 0.1%, cores 1% with no luck).
  - **Measured with `art/tools/balance/world_sim.py`** (2026-10-19): it plays a player through the world thousands of times with random drops, walking the real map (marker, node, NPC, stall and gate positions, sprinting, a 1.3 detour), fighting hit by hit (weapon damage, cooldown, hit time, enemy armour, an 85% hit rate; the enemy fights back from the first hit with its damage, windup and cooldown, knockback pushes it out of reach, armour defence and health count), healing at Roblox's default 1% of max HP a second (resting before a fight it can't take, dying costs the 5 s respawn and the walk back, a fight that would take 90% of max HP is skipped for something easier), the per-tier respawns and node respawns, quest steps in order (kills count only after accepting), shop and crafting trips, 15 s per talk with Dale. All game numbers come from `world<N>.json`, which `dump_world.luau` writes from the real configs and the map (run it in Edit mode; it requires fresh copies of the modules because Edit mode caches `require`). `--set` tries changes (`boss.PumpkinMace=0.05`, `gate.coins=2000`, `enemy.Chicken.coins=6-12`, `respawn.boss=30`, `weapon.Scythe.price=1500`, `armor.HarvestKnight.Horsehair=8`), `--trace` prints one run. Multi-target swings don't matter in World 1: markers stand 17+ studs apart (chickens 36+) and a fight pulls only the enemy hit.
  - **Results (2026-10-19):** with the Mace at 5%, a typical player takes **39 min** (fastest quarter 34, slowest quarter 47, 9 in 10 by 58.5; 5,000 runs). The Mace decides it (typical minute 36, about 23 King kills); with it guaranteed the rest takes 30 min (9 in 10 by 36) and coins run out last. Also tried: 3% 46 min, 10% 35, 5% with a 1,500-coin gate 37, 5% with a 15 s King respawn 36. The user chose 5% and kept the rest.
  - **Healing and levels (2026-10-19, the user: new players struggled, healing took too long):** with the healing fountain, level-up heals and the quicker curve (+15 HP a level, bigger armour), a typical run is **32.7 min** (fastest quarter 28.5, slowest quarter 40.6, 9 in 10 by 52; 4,000 runs). Early on a fight takes 14% of max HP on average (a bull 23%, was 36%), deaths fell from about 5.9 a run to 0.4 (0.35 in the first 10 minutes, was 1.3), and resting from 5 min to under 10 s. The simulation heals at the fountain when the detour beats resting, and on every level up. Curves tried (same EXP for every requirement): 100/50 +10 HP 34.6 min with bulls at 36%; 50/25 33.3, 31%; 30/15 +10 32.7, 28%; 30/15 +15 32.6, 23% (chosen); 25/10 32.7, 26%. `--remap` with `--set level.first= level.step= level.hp=` reruns this.
  - **Then (2026-10-19) the World 2 gate at level 25 and 10 HP a level:** typical **35.6 min** (fastest quarter 32.4, slowest quarter 44.8, 9 in 10 by 55.8; 4,000 runs). Level 25 is now the last thing ground out (about 50 Stallion kills, about 2,700 coins left over), early bull fights take 28% of HP, 0.9 deaths a run. With no sprint the same run is 38.8 min; since 2026-10-19 there is no base sprint, so that's the measured time: **39.0 min typical, 9 in 10 by 58** (the Sprint upgrade isn't counted).
  - **The target is now the gate, not the whole world (the user, 2026-10-20):** a typical free player should buy the World 2 gate in **30 minutes or more** (passes and boosts may cut that a lot; the secrets and the Mace keep their rates). `world_sim.py --goal gate` (now the default; `--goal all` is the old measure) ends the run when the gate is bought, but the player still plays normally on the way: Dale's steps, the tools, both armours when it has the level and materials, every drop, and a shop weapon whenever one it can afford hits harder per second than its best; it never chases the Mace or anything else only for completion. Re-read from Studio on 2026-10-20 (the hub stalls had moved): **36.0 min typical**, very tight (fastest quarter 35.2, 9 in 10 by 37.7), because level 25 is the last thing in (about 1,500 coins spare, 52 Stallion kills). **Kill EXP +25% (2026-10-20): Chicken 8 → 10, Bull 30 → 38, Stallion 45 → 56, Scarecrow King 120 → 150** (the Corn Wraith keeps 60): **31.6 min typical** (fastest quarter 30.6, slowest 32.9, 9 in 10 by 34.8; 5,000 runs), 39 Stallion kills, 0.57 deaths, about 1,000 coins spare, all 10 of Dale's steps and 1.6 armour sets done at the gate, levelling about every 75 s. Tried: +10% 34.0, +20% 32.3, +30% 31.1, +35% 30.4. Finishing everything (`--goal all`) is 36.8 min with it. Level 25 stays the gate (the user's question, 2026-10-20): World 2's enemies get tuned to the HP players arrive with (about 340, 415 in Harvest Knight); if World 2 needs more health, raise HP per level or its armour, not the gate level. Playtested: a chicken kill gives 10 EXP.
- **World scale: about x10 a world (2026-10-23, the user: World 1's numbers near 100, World 2 1K, World 3 10K, W4 100K ... W7 100M).** Each world's shape stays as balanced (the notes below give pre-scale numbers); on top of it every world is multiplied by its scale (`Config/Leveling`: `SCALE` {1, 4, 16, 64...} and `scaleAt(level)`, which steps up at each gate's level in `Config/Gates`, bought or not). **x SCALE** (x1 / x4 / x16): enemy damage, armour health, kill and quest EXP, and in code the player's base health and the EXP a level needs (`Leveling.maxHealth` / `need`, so level 24 has 330 HP and needs 375, level 25 1,360 and 1,560, level 50 9,440 and 12,240). **x 2 x SCALE** (x2 / x8 / x32): weapon damage, enemy health, tool power, node health, the Worldheart tiers (`Infuse.DPS` x2, x8, x32, x128...). With the worlds' own growth that is about x10 a world: weapons top out at 94 / 1,024 / 11,424 (the secrets), enemies 80..400 HP in World 1, 904..4,504 in World 2. **Coins are unscaled** (the gates already go 2,500 / 25,000); the coin bundles (`Shop.WorldScale`) and the Upgrades stand (`Upgrades.WORLD_SCALE`) went x10 a world (were 1, 4, 16 / 1, 4, 12...). World 2's hits are x3.46, not x4: Harvest Knight is World 1 armour (unscaled), so the prepared arrival is (340 x 4 + 75) x 1.15 = 1,650 HP, and every World 2 hit keeps its old share of it. **World 3 is provisional** (x32 health, x16 damage and EXP) until it is rebuilt. `world_sim.py` reads the steps from `leveling.scale` (`dump_world.luau` writes them) and has `--probe FILE` (the levels kills, quest hand-ins and worn armour happen at). Measured after: World 1 30.8 min, 0.08 deaths (unchanged); World 2 47.9 min prepared, 1.1 deaths, 53.0 unprepared, 4.4 (unchanged). Playtested: level 26 shows 1,400 / 1,400 HP and 0 / 1,620 EXP, a Dagger hits a chicken (80 HP) for 12 and crits for 18; clean console. **A saved player above level 25 drops levels** (the EXP a level needs grew x4 there); wipe test saves before launch anyway.
- **The rescale from base fights (started 2026-10-24, the user; the rules are the `world-scaling-baseline` memory).** Every world is tuned from one base fight: a player arriving maxed from the world before (World 1: level 1 with the Trowel; World 2: the W2 gate's level, Harvest Knight, upgrades 3, the Pumpkin Mace; World 3: the W3 gate's level, Heartwood Bark, upgrades 6, the Heartwood Staff; no potions or passes) loses about 50% of their health fighting the world's small enemy, and the medium / elite / boss keep today's damage and health ratios to it. A maxed player's DPS (the world's secret weapon + its upgrades, hits only, no Worldheart Blade) is about 100 / 1k / 10k; coins, EXP and prices go x10 a world; a gate every 25 levels; World 1 about 30 min to its gate, later worlds 45-60. `art/tools/balance/base_fights.py` plays the three base fights and prints the ladders and maxed DPS. **Step 1, measured before any change:** base fights 14% / 10% (the Mace's knockback makes the Fox miss) / 24%; maxed DPS 107 / 1,328 / 16,773; World 1 30.8 min, World 2 48.3 min; kill coins, stall prices and upgrades not x10 a world. **Step 2, World 1 (done):** **the level curve is x10 every 25 levels** (`Leveling.GROWTH` = 10^(1/25), +9.6% a level): max HP 100 at level 1, 229 at 10, 912 at 25, 1,000 at 26, 10,000 at 51, each level up adding a little more than the last (the user asked how health keeps up with later enemies; this keeps every world's player health x10 the last with no jump at the gate, where the old 100 + 10 a level x4 / x16 went 330 -> 1,360 at level 25), and a level needs 40 EXP at level 1, x10 every 25 levels (3,366 to level 25, was 4,860). **World 1's enemies hit 3.67x harder:** Chicken 22 (a Trowel fight now costs 50.1% on average, two pecks most fights), Bull 59, Stallion 73, Scarecrow King 81, Corn Wraith 55; health, coins, EXP, prices and level requirements unchanged. `world_sim.py`'s player now rests to 1.5x what a fight should take (`restMargin`, it only rested before a fight that would kill) and skips fights over 70% of max HP (`tooStrong`, was 90%), as a real player does with fights this costly; it reads the new curve (`leveling.growth`). Measured: **29.9 min typical** (fastest quarter 28.8, 9 in 10 by 34.7; 2,000 runs), 1.3 deaths, a chicken fight 21% of max HP on average over the run, bulls first fought around minute 16 (players level on chickens first), stallions late and easy, about 1.3 armour sets and 200 coins spare at the gate. A save's level moves with the curve (the user's 1,426 EXP is level 17, was 10). Playtested: level 17 at 961 HP (437 x the Health upgrade's 2.2), the configs read 22 / 59 / 81; clean console. **`Util.CenterRow` re-entrancy fixed the same day:** two rows of a group could refit each other forever ("Maximum re-entrancy depth (80) exceeded calling task.defer" at join); a refit passed on from the group (`fit(row, true)`) no longer passes its own change back. **Step 3, World 2 (done):** the base arrival is 1,135 HP (912 at level 25 + Harvest Knight's 75, x1.15), taking x0.795, swinging the Mace at 84.5 DPS. **Health x10 World 1's** (Fox 800, Boar 1,900, Bear 2,600, Elder Treant 4,000, Fairy 3,600); **the Fox hits for 80**, 51% over a 10 s fight (tuned without the Mace's knockback: in the game a hit shoves an enemy 10 studs and its attack only lands if it's still in reach, and the simulation's timing version of that swung a fight between 21% and 50% on a 0.1 s windup change, so the knockback is left as the Mace's bonus); the rest keep World 2's ratios (Boar 159, Bear 266, Treant 293, Fairy 199). A Fox hits 3.6x a Chicken, not x10: the arrival's Mace is 2.8x the Trowel against x10 health. **Weapons x0.8** (the Marshmallow Stick at upgrades 6: 1,328 -> 1,062 DPS; Hatchet 141, Cleaver 198, Quarterstaff 211, Mace 237, Woodsman's Axe 563, Greatclub 467, Heartwood Staff 640, Firefly Lantern 762, Marshmallow 819), the Worldheart's World 2 tier 900.9 DPS (819 x 1.1). **Prices, rewards and sells x10 World 1's:** stall weapons 1,500 / 4,000 / 6,500 / 7,000 / 18,000 / 20,000 (sells about a tenth), Woodland Hide 1,500 coins and 350 health, Heartwood Bark 7,500 and 750 (both about a quarter to a third of the level's HP), Stone Axe 500 (power 200, redwoods 1,000 hp: still 5 chops), Stone Rod 750, Rowan's rewards x10 Dale's step for step, World 2 materials and Tier I potions' sells and brews. **Kill EXP x4 and kill coins x3 of World 1's** (Fox 40 / 18-36, Boar 120 / 72-120, Bear 168 / 120-180, Treant 600 / 270-420, Fairy 240 / 75-120): x10 made World 2 a 28-minute world with 20,000 coins spare, because a maxed player kills fast; World 2 is meant to last 45-60. `world_sim.py --upgrades N` now applies **every** upgrade (Sprint everywhere, crits, regen, luck, coins, EXP, gathering; it applied only Health, Defense, Attack and Attack Speed). Measured (`--world 2 --from 1 --upgrades 3 --start-weapons PumpkinMace --start-coins 200`): **48.4 min typical** to the World 3 gate (9 in 10 by 51.9), 0.1 deaths, ~16,000 coins spare toward World 2's upgrades (97,750 for all), the Heartwood Staff in 45% of runs; most of the time is bears (about 200 kills, the best EXP a second); an unprepared arrival (no upgrades, the Spear) 64.9 min, 1.3 deaths. Playtested: the configs and a live Fox read the new numbers; clean console. **Step 4, World 3 (done):** `world3.json` dumped from the new cave for the first time (no gathering or tools yet; its gate is the mock World 4 gate below). The base arrival is 12,831 HP (9,120 at level 50 + Heartwood Bark's 750, x1.3), taking x0.639, swinging the Heartwood Staff at 754 DPS. **Health x10 World 2's** (Cave Bat 8,000, Pebble Golem 19,000, Crystal Golem 26,000, Spider Queen 40,000, Gem Mole 36,000); **the Bat hits for 1,220** (50% over an 11 s fight; the base fights leave knockback out); the rest keep World 3's ratios (2,460 / 4,090 / 4,500, Mole 3,070). **Weapons x0.63** (the Rock Candy Club at upgrades 9: 16,773 -> 10,567 DPS; Batwing Blade 1,230 ... Rock Candy Club 7,197), the Worldheart's tier 3 7,916.7 DPS. **Prices, rewards and sells x10 World 2's** (stall 15,000 / 40,000 / 65,000 / 70,000 / 180,000 / 200,000; Cave Runner level 53 (58 since the same day, below), 15,000 coins, 3,500 health (5,500); Crystal Queen level 59 (65), 75,000, 7,500 (13,000); Pip's rewards x10 Rowan's; World 3 materials and Tier II potions). **Pip's steps moved for a level-50 arrival:** level steps 55 / 59 (were 36 / 42; 60 / 65 since the same day, below), earn 2,000 (800), give 10,000 (1,500), dialogue too. **The Rock Candy Club drops at 0.1%** (the secrets' rule; it was still 2%). **Kill EXP and coins x5.5 World 2's** (Bat 220 / 100-200, Pebble 660 / 400-660, Crystal 920 / 660-990, Queen 3,300 / 1,500-2,300, Mole 1,320 / 410-660). **The World 4 gate is a mock** (a comment in `Config/Gates`, not a real gate yet): level 75, 250,000 coins, and about 30 + 5 World 3 gathered materials once World 3 has gathering. Measured (`--world 3 --from 2 --upgrades 6 --start-level 50 --start-weapons HeartwoodStaff --start-armor WoodlandHide,HeartwoodBark --start-wear HeartwoodBark --start-coins 1000`): **50.3 min typical** (9 in 10 by 51.4), no deaths, both level and coins binding; unprepared (no upgrades, the Firefly Lantern) 80.5 min. Pip's questline is done by about minute 9; the rest is mostly Crystal Golems (about 234 kills; World 2's end is about 200 bears the same way). `base_fights.py` reads `world3.json` and leaves the base weapon's knockback out. Playtested: the configs and a live Bat read the new numbers; clean console. **The rescale's three base fights now all cost about 50% (50.1 / 51.2 / 50.1%) and maxed DPS is 107 / 1,062 / 10,567.** **The reward ladder (2026-10-24, the user: spread the elite grind; elites stay the best way to level, the boss pays a hefty amount but isn't the best to farm, since its draw is its weapon drops):** per kill, in elites, for EXP and coins alike: **small 0.37, medium 0.7, elite 1, boss 4.3**, the secret enemy 1.4; then each world's whole ladder scaled to its time (World 1 0.75x the old Stallion, Worlds 2 and 3 0.95x the old Bear / Crystal Golem). Per second for a player mid to late in the world (`art/tools/balance/rates_probe.py`, early / mid / late cases): small 64-73%, medium 72-84%, **elite 100%**, boss 77-95% (it has one spot, a long fight and a 30 s respawn, so 3 elites a kill paid only ~60% a second; early in a world the boss pays the most but isn't safe to fight yet). New rewards: Chicken 12 EXP / 9-18 coins, Bull 22 / 18-35, Stallion 32 / 25-50, Scarecrow King 135 / 110-215, Corn Wraith 45 / 35-70; Fox 60 / 35-70, Boar 110 / 65-135, Bear 160 / 95-190, Elder Treant 690 / 410-815, Fairy 225 / 135-265; Cave Bat 320 / 195-385, Pebble Golem 610 / 370-730, Crystal Golem 875 / 525-1,045, Spider Queen 3,760 / 2,260-4,480, Gem Mole 1,225 / 735-1,460. `world_sim.py`'s player now takes the nearest of anything paying within `closeEnough` (10%) of the best (a real player doesn't cross the map for a few percent). Measured: **30.0 / 48.6 / 49.6 min**; a min-maxer still farms the elite, while a player who fights whatever pays within 25% spreads the kills (World 2: 171 boars, 102 bears) and takes 30.7 / 52.9 / 56.1 min.
- **World 3 remodel, started 2026-10-23 (the user's design; plan in the `world3-caverns` memory, drawing `art/tools/w3/layout.py`).** The cave mouth and crystal gate stay; past them a 17-wide passage runs 12 studs east and curves 90 degrees south (radius 74, 128 long) going down 30 studs in 1-stud steps (~13 degrees), into a cave the size of Worlds 1 and 2 running south: x 122..386, z -1006..-1522, floor y -22, the same halls and lane loops as before stretched (the middle hall grew most), ceilings 28 in the lanes and stepped domes of 40 / 45 / 50 in the entry, middle and boss halls. **The floor is flat with rough ground along the walls** (the user): blocky 1-stud patches only in a band 10-20 studs out from the rock. **The shell is built** (`W03…/Ground/Floor` (`Floor` + `Floor_1`), `Walls` (`Ledge` / `Overhang` / `Rock`), `Ceiling`; 3,774 parts) by `art/tools/w3/build_shell.py`, which writes world-aligned boxes; they reach Studio as a temporary Rojo module `src/shared/DevData/W3Shell.luau` (not committed; delete it, file and Studio copy, when World 3 is done) built by a one-off `execute_luau`. Checked: the floor runs on from the mouth's at y 8 with no gap (x 166), rays up and out from 1,734 floor spots all hit rock (no hole to the sky), and a walk through the curve and both lanes of both loops into the boss hall never stuck. **The old World 3 is parked whole as `Workspace._W3Old`, 3,000 studs east** (its `Offset` attribute), tags and all, so its stalls, altar, Pip, mineshafts, secret room, crystals, stalagmites, ores and tracks can be moved into the new cave; delete what's left once the user approves. **The way down is a blocky arch, not a smooth vault (the user):** the cave mouth's 14 angled `Vault` slabs are gone (backup `ServerStorage/MapBackup_2026-10-23/W2CaveMouthVaultBefore`, with the old columns and floor); its 17 stepped `Rock/Lower` columns over the passage are the visible ceiling now (57536b), on the same rule as the passage's (`build_shell.arch`: springing 12 over the floor, radius = the half-width, 17 at the mouth, measured at each 2-stud column's inner edge and rounded up), so every step sits 0.6-1.4 studs outside the stone arch's inner circle (radius 16.4) and none shows from outside; the mouth's `CaveFloor` runs to x 168 and the build grid sits on odd z, so the passage's columns meet the mouth's exactly (same roof height on both sides of x 168 in every column). Its Amount's UIPadding holds FontDrop's correction (0.139 / -0.139) so Edit mode shows it centred, as do the player list rows' Level / Coins. `IconRing` (a cream circle with an ink rim, the full height) holds a big `CoinIcon`, and `Panel` is a dark card-style bar (343242, the bars' ink rim) running from the ring's middle with a gold Luckiest Guy count (`CoinsUI` runs `FontDrop.watch` on it). **The card is bigger (the user: too small to read):** `TopLeft` is 0.2025 of the screen tall (was 0.1775) and 0.53 wide (was 0.455; the bars a decent bit longer: Health 0.95 and EXP 0.86 of `Info`, about 413 / 374 px at 1153 tall, were 323 / 291); the `LevelBadge` is square with its FredokaOne number centred (no drop: FredokaOne draws centred; since 2026-10-23 PlayerCardUI also shifts it sideways by the digits' measured centre of mass, `GLYPHS`, because a "1" read as pushed right, then puts the label's corner on a whole pixel: Roblox draws text that starts part-way into a pixel from the next one, which put every number about 1 px right and 1.2 px low at the HUD's size. Measured after, at real size: "1" / "25" / "100" within 0.7 / 0.3 / 0.4 px both ways; `PlayerCardUI.watchBadge` does the same for the profile and inspect cards' badges). The name above the bars has a `Lift` UIPadding (top -0.16, bottom +0.16 of its box) so its letters sit about 3.5 px higher, clear of the Health bar (the user, 2026-10-23) and widens to the left only once the number doesn't fit (PlayerCardUI's `fitBadge` sets its UIAspectRatioConstraint, DominantAxis Height, to max(`MinAspect` 1, the number's drawn width x `Slack` 1.12 / the label's 0.86 share), read from the label's own `TextBounds` shape: square to 99, 1.03 at 100, 1.58 at 1,000; anchored on its right edge); and in `PlayerCard/Panel/Info` Health is 0.35 and EXP 0.28 tall (48 and 39 px at 1080, were 39 and 27) with a 0.085 gap, their labels 0.54 of the bar. **The Worlds menu stops at the worlds you have** (the user): `WorldsUI.reachable()` = HighestWorld; Prev / Next don't wrap, stop at world 1 and the last unlocked one and fade there (`Art` 0.55, inactive), and a locked world's dot doesn't respond. **Leaderboard podium:** `Podium` 0.31 tall (the `List` moved down to 0.515); each `Place` has a gradient `Block` (gold / silver / bronze) with a white outlined `Rank` and the `Value` on it, `PlayerName` above, an `Avatar` circle (LeaderboardUI fills the row's `rbxthumb` headshot) and on `Place1` a `Crown` (`rbxassetid://89009378741260`, `art/tools/ui/podium_ribbon.html` -> `art/ui/leaderboard/podium_crown.png`). **Worlds title:** a gold `Ribbon` (`129802809389990`, `art/ui/worlds/worlds_ribbon.png`, same generator) across the picture's top edge holding `Title`; `Close` moved to the picture's top-right corner. **Robux shop titles:** the header `Title` centred on the band (the Close button's middle) with a UIPadding, and the three section-bar titles' boxes centred in their bars, so `FontDrop` centres the letters. stacks with passes), Health (+5% max HP), Defense (-1% damage taken, after armour and Stoneskin), Attack (+3% weapon damage), Attack Speed (-1.5% swing time, through `Upgrades.adjust` beside `Infuse.adjust`, client and server), Regen (+0.15% of max HP a second a level on top of Roblox's own 1%; +0.25% until 2026-10-22), Coins (+4% kill coins), EXP (+4% kill EXP), Gathering (since 2026-10-22, the user: a 1%-a-level chance that each drop of a tree or rock comes doubled, 0% at level 0; it was x1.1 materials a level, and before that a +10% chance of 1 extra), and since 2026-10-22 **Crit Damage** (+0.05 a level on the crit multiplier, from `Balance.Melee.critMultiplier` 1.5) and **Crit Chance** (+1% a level, from `Balance.Melee.critChance` 10%; +0.15% a level now)"). **3 levels per world unlocked** (`Upgrades.cap(HighestWorld)`), up to 24; prices `STEP_PRICES` 100 / 250 / 500 x `WORLD_SCALE` (1, 4, 12...), Sprint half (placeholders). Each system reads its level through `Upgrades.value(player, id)`: SprintController, MeleeService (Attack, Defense, Attack Speed), WeaponController (Attack Speed), ExpService (Health, EXP), CoinService (Coins), IndexService + MaterialService + IndexUI's shown chances (Luck), GatherService (Gathering).
